# HVAC round 2: the master channel list (2026-10-24)

> **Checker notes (2026-10-24, `check-master.md`): the tiers here are provisional and often wrong.** On 86 channels checked against their own Videos tabs, stored tier A was wrong for 20 of 30, B for 11 of 15, C for 8 of 15; D (1 of 10) and X (0 of 10) held up. Cause: most rows were tiered from keyword-search hits, which return only a channel's HVAC videos, so big general channels with old AC hits look HVAC-first. Round 3 re-tiers every A-D channel from its own latest 30 long-form videos with the rule in `check-master.md`. Subscribers and country matched the About pages for all 86.

Helper: round 2 master-list helper. Built 2026-10-24. Nothing committed or pushed. **Not yet re-checked by a second helper (working rule 3).**

**What this is:** every channel found so far in the HVAC study (round 1 and round 2), merged into one list, one row per YouTube channel ID, and tiered so round 3 (the census) knows which channels to pull full metrics for. Full table, every column, every tier: `studies/hvac/data/raw/channels-master-2026-10-24.csv`.

**NexLev RPM is not used** (operator, 2026-10-24). No NexLev revenue or RPM figure is in this list. NexLev's outlier score and first-video date are carried in the CSV only where an earlier helper pulled them.

**Readings vs measurements.** Subscribers, total views, joined dates, video counts and view counts are measured (each with its source and pull date in the CSV). **Tier, type, audience and main topics are readings** (an earlier helper's or mine, marked in the CSV columns `tier_basis`, `type_source`, `audience_source`). "not found" = no source gave it.

## Result

**1,941 channels** in the master list.

| Tier | Rule (my reading, applied to every channel) | Channels |
|---|---|---:|
| A | HVAC-first for homeowners: HVAC is the channel's main subject (AC, heat pumps, furnaces, boilers, thermostats, filters, ducts, the heating / cooling bill). | 157 |
| B | General home DIY / home repair / handyman, with a real HVAC, insulation or bill share (not the main subject). | 244 |
| C | Adjacent lanes: off-grid and supplemental heat (wood, pellet, diesel, waste oil, "free heat"), outage / blackout, energy and insulation, product and brand verdict lists, frugal living, old-ways heating and cooling history. | 379 |
| D | Trade-facing (technicians, students, HVAC business) or a brand / contractor / retailer / manufacturer channel. | 342 |
| X | Off-niche: car AC, PC cooling, Indian / Pakistani-market AC, electronics, gaming, sport, news, sleep sounds, fan collectors, camping, single viral hits. Kept in the CSV, dropped from the census. | 819 |
| | **Census (A to D)** | **1,122** |

Flags: **NEW_12M** (joined on or after 2025-10-24, or a restart in that window) = 165 channels in tiers A-D (A 49, B 6, C 102, D 8); 34 more in X. **PERSONA** (faceless persona or AI avatar, by an earlier type reading) = 66 in tiers A-D (A 27, B 1, C 38, D 0). Joined date not found for 2 A-D channels, so NEW_12M is a floor.

## Method

### 1. Sources

| Source | File(s) | What was taken | Channels in the master list from it | Only in this source |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|
| known8 (`K8`) | `seeds/known-channels-recheck.md (s.1-2)` | the 8 known channels, YT figures 2026-10-23, type readings, top video | 8 | 0 |
| known8-leads (`K8L`) | `seeds/known-channels-recheck.md (s.4 Leads)` | 23 lead rows (25 channels; 3 by handle only, resolved below) | 25 | 4 |
| round1 (`R1`) | `seeds/channel-finds-round1.md, data/raw/channel-finds-round1.csv` | 174 channels with fit, audience, type, About figures, example video | 174 | 9 |
| round1-rejected (`R1X`) | `seeds/channel-finds-round1.md ("left out")` | 19 rejected candidates (names only; IDs matched by exact name in the round 1 raw JSON) | 19 | 7 |
| leads-hvac (`UN`) | `seeds/leads-channels.md` | 129 HVAC / partly HVAC up-next and similar-video leads | 129 | 8 |
| leads-nonhvac (`UNX`) | `seeds/leads-channels.md` | 20 non-HVAC channels YouTube pairs with the seeds | 20 | 15 |
| r2-nexlev (`NX2`) | `round2/nexlev-channels.md, data/raw/round2-nexlev-channels.csv` | 164 channels with fit, audience, type, NexLev flags, About figures | 164 | 30 |
| oldheat-heat (`OHH`) | `round2/old-house-heat.md, data/raw/round2-oldheat-2026-10-24.json (pool_heat)` | channels of the 380 kept old-house heat videos | 212 | 44 |
| oldheat-ac (`OHA`) | `same JSON (pool_ac)` | channels of the 315 kept AC videos in the helper's matched comparison set | 190 | 15 |
| sweep (`SW`) | `round2/keyword-sweep.md, data/raw/sweep-channels-2026-10-24.csv, sweep-videos-2026-10-24.csv` | channels passing the rule below (and the sweep counts of every listed channel) | 1,809 | 1,175 |

Silver Cymbal (`UC67dKTEwpAhKv2oYDATwR8g`) is in round 1's table and the sweep, so it is in the list; it is tiered B (on-camera general home DIY with a large HVAC share; reading).

The old-house heat helper's AC pool is a set of AC channels it pulled only to compare with heat. I included its channels too ("every channel found so far"); 15 of them are found by no other source.

### 2. The sweep rule

The sweep returned 25,618 distinct long-form English videos from 13,708 channel rows; 16,270 of the videos have a title that passes the HVAC filter (`is_hvac` in `data/tools/autocomplete.py`, as already applied in `sweep-videos-2026-10-24.csv`, column `hvac_word_in_title`).

A sweep channel is included when **(a)** it has **3 or more distinct returned long-form videos whose title passes the HVAC filter**, or **(b)** it has **at least one returned HVAC-title video with 500,000+ views**.

| Rule | Channels meeting it | Of these, not found by any other source (added by the sweep alone) |
|---|---:|---:|
| (a) 3+ HVAC-title videos | 987 | 655 |
| (b) a 500K+ HVAC-title video, and fewer than 3 HVAC-title videos | 557 | 520 |
| (both a and b) | 295 | (counted in a) |
| **Total included from the sweep** | **1,544** | **1,175** |

Collaboration videos: the sweep CSV leaves the channel ID empty when a video is a collaboration ("John The HVAC Guy and 9 more"); 373 sweep videos are like that. I credited each to its uploader (the oEmbed `author`, pulled by the sweep helper) when that name matched a channel already in the sources or resolved by search (below): 221 credited, 152 not. That lifted 18 channels over the 3-video line that the sweep CSV's own count (`hvac_word_videos`, which skips collabs) leaves below it. The CSV keeps both: `sweep_hvac_title_videos` (with collabs) and the note.

Reading: rule (b) is wide. Many of its channels are one viral video on a channel about something else ("Hot vs Cold Room Challenge", NBA "Heat", "Furnace" in a game). They are kept and tiered X, as the rule asks.

### 3. IDs, duplicates and overlap

- Every source row was keyed by channel ID. Rows with no ID: 3 handle-only leads (`@PrimeRundown`, `@HullHonest`, `@GrillVerdict`), resolved from each handle's YouTube page (`channelMetadataRenderer`); collaboration rows in the old-house heat file ("Vent Exposed and 3 more", "Sylvie Mercer and 3 more" ...), credited to the lead channel by name.
- Names searched on YouTube's channel search, accepted only on one exact-name match: 13 looked up, **12 resolved**, 1 not ("Handy Leo": several channels have that exact name; left unresolved, so the one old-house heat video `QBTgvGGHl1U` (281 views) is not in the list). Script `data/tools/master_resolve.py`; results `data/raw/channels-master-resolve-2026-10-24.json` (pulled 2026-10-24). No NexLev `channel_resolver` call was needed.
- The 19 round-1 rejected names were matched to IDs by exact name inside round 1's raw JSON (`channel-finds-nexlev-2026-10-23.json`, `channel-finds-ytsearch-*.json`); each About title was then checked (problems section).
- Duplicates merged: 2,751 source records collapsed into 1,941 channel IDs. Every source that found a channel is kept in the `sources` column (codes in the table above) and, where the source said how, in `found_by_detail`.

### 4. Merging and gap-filling

- **Most recent pull wins.** For subscribers, total views, joined date, video count and country, every candidate value was collected (About panels pulled by the round 1, up-next, round 2 NexLev and old-house heat helpers; the source tables; my own About pulls) and the latest pull kept, with its source in the `*_source` columns. Where another source differs by more than 5% (or a different joined date), the CSV `conflicts` column gives both (4 channels).
- **About pages for the gaps.** 1,237 channels had no subscriber / joined / video-count figure in any source. Their YouTube About panels were pulled logged out (`data/tools/master_about.py`, the `yt_water1.py about` method, about 1 s between channels, 4 workers, 2026-10-24); raw panels in `data/raw/channels-master-about-2026-10-24.json`. **1,237 returned figures**; 0 did not (no About panel, a terminated or missing channel, or a parse error). Still not found after this: subscribers for 11 channels (10 in tiers A-D), joined date for 2 (2 in A-D).
- **Type** comes from the earlier helpers' readings (known 8, round 1, round 2 NexLev, the old-house heat faceless table). For channels no one had looked at, it is "not checked" (round 3 looks at thumbnails), except channels tiered D whose name is plainly a company ("... Heating & Air", "... LLC", "... Supply"): those are "brand" (my reading from the name). Type categories: on camera / faceless / AI avatar / faceless persona / brand / not checked; the earlier wording is kept in `type_detail`.
- **Best known HVAC video:** the highest-view HVAC-scope video any source recorded for the channel (the known-channel top video, round 1 / round 2 example video, the up-next example, the old-house heat or AC pool video, or the sweep's biggest HVAC-title video). The same video seen in several pulls keeps the latest pull's count. Up-next figures are YouTube's rounded short text ("763K") and are kept as given.
- **Sweep columns** (every listed channel the sweep returned): videos returned, HVAC-title videos returned (with collabs), their total views, and how many of them have 500K+ views.
- **Audience:** earlier helpers' reading where they gave one; otherwise mine from the titles (A-C homeowner unless stated; D trade, or mixed for brand channels).
- **NEW_12M:** joined date on or after 2025-10-24 (About panel), or a restart: John The HVAC Guy (2020 account, first HVAC video 2026-08-13, per the known-channels re-check) and round 2 channels whose NexLev first-video date is in the window while the account is older (NexLev dates read as month only).
- **PERSONA:** type is faceless persona or AI avatar. Only channels an earlier helper typed can carry it, so it is a floor: most sweep-only channels are "not checked".

### 5. How tiers were read

| Basis | Channels |
|---|---:|
| my reading of the returned titles and channel name (2026-10-24) | 1,565 |
| from the R1 fit tag '...' | 159 |
| from the NX2 fit tag '...' | 158 |
| up-next lead marked non-HVAC | 20 |
| rejected in round 1 (kept as rejected) | 18 |
| my reading of an ADJ / rejected-then-kept channel | 12 |
| known HVAC channel (fit HVAC) | 8 |
| Silver Cymbal (study seed): on-camera general home DIY; HVAC and insulation are a large share | 1 |

Earlier fit tags were mapped as: HVAC -> A (D if the earlier audience was "trade"), DIY -> B, ENERGY / OUTAGE / OFFGRID -> C, TRADE / BRAND -> D; ADJ channels were read one by one (12). Round-1 rejects stay X (Patina is the exception: round 2 re-found and kept it, so the latest reading, C, wins). For the 1,565 channels no earlier helper had tagged, I read each one's returned titles (up to 5 HVAC-title and other titles, with views) and its name; these readings are in the build script inputs and the CSV says which basis each row has.

## Corrections carried in from the round 2 checker (2026-10-24, relayed by the main session)

1. HVACTIME (`UCRdNcv3g_4rpbNEyFopZwDg`), Adrian Garcia / @ReliableHVACR (`UCKDkGhLRlzs1bV1tmeokcNQ`) and Stephen Shares HVAC Tips at Goettl (`UCcrs-w2r-S3VR7pe8t9DIew`) are **on camera** (round 2 had "faceless, real footage").
2. Every type taken from `round2/nexlev-channels.md` was read from one thumbnail: those rows say `NX2 (provisional)` in `type_source` and "[provisional ...]" in `type_detail`.
3. Adrian Garcia's round 2 example video (a tool bag) is not used as his best HVAC video; his best is now the sweep's.
4. TheDIYGuyDOTnet's "How To Remove Air From Your Heating System" (`z80WspC4Nmg`, 2,050,577 views) belongs in the old-house heat set (missed by that helper's rule). The channel was already in the list (old-house heat pool and sweep, tier A) with this video as its best; a note is added.
5. Halden Row (`UC5knbXUbhA0MAGcQNM1SkKA`) is "faceless, AI images", not a persona (no PERSONA flag).
6. Hindi titles with an English tail can pass the sweep's English rule (e.g. `iMyniW0gJcQ`, INFORMATION TYAGI). I checked every returned title of every A-D channel for non-Latin script and for Hindi/Urdu words in Latin letters: none of the A-D channels rests on such titles (the three hits were "UPPÅTVIND", a "ΔT" and a stray symbol in English titles). The Indian- and Pakistani-market channels the sweep pulled in were already read as X.

## Problems and limits

- **Not yet re-checked by a second helper** (working rule 3). The 1,565 tier readings for channels no earlier helper had tagged are mine, from up to 5 returned titles plus the name; a checker should sample them, most of all the A/B and C/X borders.
- **Type is mostly unread.** 84 of 157 tier-A channels and 249 of 379 tier-C channels are "not checked": the sweep and old-house heat sources carry no type. PERSONA (66 in A-D) is therefore a floor; round 3's thumbnail look will add more.
- **The sweep's rule (b) is wide** (one 500K+ HVAC-title video): it brings in sport ("Heat"), games ("Furnace"), kids' "hot vs cold" videos and news. They are kept and tiered X.
- **The HVAC title filter is strict and imperfect** (round 2 sweep note): it misses some on-topic titles (any title with "hours") and passes some off-topic ones (a car "thermostat", a game "furnace"). Counts in the sweep columns are counts of that filter, not of my reading.
- **Collaboration videos:** 152 of the sweep's 373 collab videos could not be credited to a channel (the uploader name matched nothing listed), so a few small channels may sit just under the 3-video line.
- **"Handy Leo"** (one old-house heat video, `QBTgvGGHl1U`, 281 views) is not in the list: several channels share that exact name.
- **About panels:** every one of the 1,237 channels I pulled returned figures (6 failed once and worked on the retry). Subscriber counts are YouTube's rounded figures ("1.48K"), stored as numbers. Hidden subscriber counts: 11 channels still "not found" (Home Fix Garage, Hal's Boiler Room, HomeFix Guide, The House Papers, fixmyownac, The Technician's Blacklist, Old House Walt, Dale's Winter Fixes, Ray Holton Secrets, Michaels HVAC Service, homesealni); joined date not found for 2.
- **Conflicts** are small: 4 joined dates one day apart between About pulls of 10-23 and 10-24 (likely the time zone of the "Joined" text). The latest pull is kept.
- **Renamed channels:** 18 About titles differ from the name a source recorded (for example "MR.YOU NOT QUALIFIED" now shows as "AARON THE PLUMBER OFFICIAL YOUTUBE CHANNEL", "Estadistica Datos del Mundo" as "Sewing with Ulyana"); the ID is the same, the About name is used, and the CSV `notes` keeps the old one.
- **Country** is the About panel's own field; many channels give none ("not found").
- **The AC pool** of the old-house heat helper was included (190 channels; 15 found nowhere else). If the operator meant only its heat channels, those 15 can be dropped by the `sources` column (`OHA` alone).
- **Best known HVAC video** is the biggest HVAC-scope video any source happened to record, not the channel's true top video; round 3's Popular-tab pull will replace it.
- **NEW_12M** is a joined-date test; it does not judge "taking off" (deliverable 2), which needs round 3's video pulls.

## Files

- `studies/hvac/round2/channels-master.md`: this file.
- `studies/hvac/data/raw/channels-master-2026-10-24.csv`: the full list, every tier, every column (tier and its basis, NEW_12M and its basis, PERSONA, name, handle, ID, link, type / type detail / type source, NexLev flags, persona or About text, audience and its source, subs / total views / joined / video count / country each with source, main topics, best known HVAC video with source, sweep inclusion rule and counts, old-house heat counts, NexLev outlier and first-video date where pulled, sources, found-by detail, conflicts, notes).
- `studies/hvac/data/raw/channels-master-about-2026-10-24.json`: the 1,237 About panels pulled for the gaps (UTC time on each).
- `studies/hvac/data/raw/channels-master-resolve-2026-10-24.json`: handle and name lookups.
- Scripts (`studies/hvac/data/tools/`): `master_collect.py` (reads every source, applies the sweep rule, credits collab videos), `master_resolve.py` (handle / name to ID), `master_about.py` (About panels), `master_build.py` (merge, most-recent-wins, types, tiers, flags, the CSV; holds the checker corrections and the ADJ readings), `master_report.py` (this file). 
- `studies/hvac/data/raw/channels-master-tier-readings-2026-10-24.csv`: my 1,565 per-channel tier readings (the input `master_build.py` used, with IDs and names).
